    - Path 1: A → B → G → H
    - Path 2: A → B → G → C → N → O
    - Path 3: A → B → T → F → Q → I → P
    - Path 4: A → B → E → F → S → Q → I → P

    - Any path from B (neighbor of A) to H (black node) will require passing through B, G, or C.
    - Removing all paths requires cutting the connections from B to the paths that lead to H. The minimal cut set requires 2 edges to disconnect all useful paths from B to H.

6. **Checking the edges to remove:** If we consider the paths formed by shared common edge and dependent connectedness.

Image name: o+_task3_gt3_PAIR1
Task: task3_min_edges_removal
Model: mistral-small-3.1-24b
Mode: few_shot, COT: cot, Attempt: 4
--- Model Response ---
To answer the question, we need to identify the minimum number of edges that must be removed to completely disconnect the two black nodes, which are J and R in the provided graph.

### Steps to Analyze the Graph:

1. **Identify the two black nodes:**
   The two black nodes are J and R.

2. **Identify the immediate neighboring nodes:**
   - J's immediate neighboring nodes are O, I, and R.
   - R's immediate neighboring nodes are J, I, G, and C.

3. **Trace the paths between the two black nodes:**
   - From J, the paths go through O, I, and R directly.
   - From R, the paths go through J, I, G, and C.

4. **Determine the interactions and connections:**
   - J connects to R directly.
   - J connects to I, which also connects to R.
   - J connects to O, which has no direct connection to R or its neighbors.
   - R connects to G, which connects to H, B, and T. B connects to E, S, A, and F. T connects to B and F. None of these nodes connect back to J.
   - R connects to C, which connects to N. N has no direct connection to J or its neighbors.

5. **Find the minimal cut set:**
   - To disconnect J and R, we need to remove the direct edge between them.
